Production surface: A team in one business line requesting a model for one workflow · The ThinkLatent Portal: request, approved access, served model · Days from request to approved access on the first five workflows

Governance (separate team): not started, Usage-approval record aligned with Risk's model inventory — on the critical path

### Decisions due
_None._

### Hypotheses
| Claim | Confirmed if | Refuted if | Evidence | Confidence | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| P1. A single front door shortens adoption | Median time falls by half on the first five workflows onboarded through the portal | No measurable change, or teams route around the portal | not set | not set | open |
| P2. Usage-based approval cuts governance rework | Second and later usages of an approved model clear review materially faster | Governance still runs bespoke reviews for each usage | not set | not set | open |
| P3. SLM teams reuse instead of rebuild | The second SLM build on the portal reuses harness, bars and serving with no new eval scaffolding | Each build still needs its own scaffolding | not set | not set | open |
| P4. Demand signals steer the foundation roadmap | At least one roadmap decision in two quarters cites portal demand data | No roadmap decision cites it | not set | not set | open |

### Kill signals
_None recorded._

<details><summary><strong>TL Index</strong> · TL-003.index · Building · active · due 2026-11-20</summary>

**Purpose.** TL Index: a private, versioned evaluation layer that scores AI models on sixteen Enterprise-relevant capability pillars and on end-to-end use cases built from our own tools, and prices every score against cost and deployability. Its first job is to test whether our workload needs frontier models by default; its lasting job is to be the instrument every model decision at the Enterprise runs through: selection for a workflow, adoption of a new release, in-house training targets, production swaps and vendor spend (the proposal's executive summary).

**Merged** 2026-11-16 from [TL-004 index](TL-004-index.md) (D-388). Stage at merge: TL-004 Building, score 4.1 set.

**Owner.** Index lead (Claude, playing the Research function, in chats kept apart from build work and from TLM model-building, D-155 kept by D-388); Harness and platform lead (Claude, as the S-B stream engineer); Head of ThinkLatent (Jithin), sponsor, decider and the practitioner who sets the bars. Stream S-B; appetite 10 engineer-weeks, ends 2027-01-24; review Fridays 30 min, shared with the portal's weekly review (D-388); critique recorded.

**Next step** (due 2026-11-20). Merged into the portal on 16 Nov (D-388); D-155 kept, so the index's judging stays apart from TLM model-building and only where its screens live changed (D-238). Review: Fridays 30 min, now also the portal's weekly review. Decision spike week 1 from 2026-11-16 under S-B (Claude as index lead and harness lead): pick the three bounded workflows on public-data proxies, run three model classes by hand, with Jithin setting the bars as the practitioner; first Friday review 2026-11-20. Share the proposal with the team (a recorded red-team pass plus Jithin's comments); rename the artifact's browser title on claude.ai first, since it still carries the old name. From TL-003 (2026-11-02): the per-use-case line item should run against the endpoint (the served system) once harnesses exist; the Judge domain model and the judge-location question are one decision; scout-brief-eval-governance.md (2026-11-02) lists ten presentation features for the index (intervals and n per score, item drill-down, Official versus Practice runs, judge calibration cards, sufficiency view). From TL-012 (2026-11-02): add the variant comparison card (variants against rubric, cost per task, latency, pass^k, a frontier baseline, the team's choice logged) to the index's presentation features; TL-012's H3, H4, H7 and H8 run on the index. From TL-016 (2026-11-05, scout-brief-team-site.md): add a living evaluations hub with method and exclusions stated, an index-as-artifact page (ranked graphic, method, data, plain-language explainer), an annual report hub (takeaways, chapters, leadership digest, data download, recorded launch, archive), a corrections log, the leaderboard as its own section, and a source and as-of date on every number; the site carries a TL Index summary tile and links here From the portal session of 11 Nov (D-236): decide on the pillars proposed for a later edition (security and misuse resistance, privacy and data handling, robustness, numeracy and tables, attribution, fair treatment); the portal shows them as proposed and unscored. From the portal session of 11 Nov (D-238): Jithin decided the index is built inside the portal from now on; recorded 16 Nov with the merge, D-155 kept (D-388). Also from D-238: a preference rating from blind votes; a Real Enterprise category with a score per use case (the team ask is artifacts/TL-003-portal/enterprise-benchmark-team-ask-2026-11-11.md); the proposal's token-shape matrix now has a first form as the portal's size map. From TL-003's build of 2026-11-11: the portal's Trends page has one open-weights and one frontier point; run two earlier open-weights models and last year's frontier aggregate on edition 2026-11 as Official runs so each class has a line. From TL-022 (2026-11-15): the fast lane needs the index to gate 4-bit weights against FP8 on service-level tasks (H3) and to say whether the 7B Core is sufficient for the 200 ms class (H4).

**Production surface.** Head of ThinkLatent, model owners, Risk reviewers · The ThinkLatent Portal's evidence layer (register API) · Model decisions and governance approvals made through the index per quarter

**Governance (separate team).** not started, Judge-panel location and training-data wall sign-off

**Decisions due.**
_None._

**Hypotheses.**

| Claim | Confirmed if | Refuted if | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H1. Task complexity, not model size, predicts fit for Enterprise tasks | Most pillars plateau at a mid-size or small model class; score correlates with task complexity more than with model size | Scores keep rising with model size across most pillars, including bounded tasks | open |
| H2. Frontier upgrades do not move Enterprise-relevant metrics | Public benchmark gains are large while TL Index pillar movement is within noise, except on named reasoning-heavy pillars | Each release moves several TL Index pillars past the production bar where the incumbent fell short | open |
| H3. Task-specific trained smaller models beat general models in Enterprise context | Specialists win on bounded pillars (1, 3-routing, 7, 14, 15) at lower cost, and the gap holds across two quarterly re-runs | General models match specialists at similar cost, or specialist gains erode between re-runs faster than retraining is affordable | open |

**Kill criteria.**

| Criterion | Checked | Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The four-week decision spike finds no bounded workflow where a mid-size or small model clears a practitioner-set bar | End of the spike | Re-scope the TL Index to a regression gate and release-evaluation service; drop the training-targets claim |
| Fewer than three business partners have signed a production bar | End of Phase 1 | Park; the index has no authority |
| The harness cannot reproduce a public benchmark within published error bars | Phase 0 exit | Stop until fixed; nothing is published from an uncalibrated harness |
| No sufficiency decision for at least two real workflows within two quarters of Phase 1 start | Two quarters after Phase 1 start | Kill, or re-scope to the regression gate only |
| No model decision cites the index in the two quarters after the first report | Two quarters after Phase 2 | Park; the product is not used |
| Any task-library item or near-duplicate found in a training batch | Any audit | Freeze both programmes until the wall is rebuilt and audited |
| Index lead and harness lead roles not assigned | Spike start | Do not start the spike or Phase 0 |

**Kill signals.**

| Signal | By | Status | Action |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The four-week decision spike finds no bounded workflow where a mid-size or small model clears a practitioner-set bar | no date | open | Re-scope the TL Index to a regression gate and release-evaluation service; drop the training-targets claim |
| Fewer than three business partners have signed a production bar | no date | open | Park; the index has no authority |
| The harness cannot reproduce a public benchmark within published error bars | no date | open | Stop until fixed; nothing is published from an uncalibrated harness |
| No sufficiency decision for at least two real workflows within two quarters of Phase 1 start | no date | open | Kill, or re-scope to the regression gate only |
| No model decision cites the index in the two quarters after the first report | no date | open | Park; the product is not used |
| Any task-library item or near-duplicate found in a training batch | no date | open | Freeze both programmes until the wall is rebuilt and audited |
| Index lead and harness lead roles not assigned | no date | open | Do not start the spike or Phase 0 |

**Open questions.**

| Question | Owner | Blocking |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Which business-line leaders own the production bar for each MVP pillar? | Pillar owners | Yes |
| Where does the judge panel run, given that frontier judges may sit outside our boundary? | Security and Risk liaisons | Yes |
| Do we license an external finance benchmark as a reference pillar, or stay fully private? | Index lead with Procurement | No |
| What retraining cadence are we willing to fund for specialists, since that cost belongs in H3? | Training programme liaison with Finance | Yes, for H3 |
| Which business-line weights define the composites, and who signs them off? | Sponsor with business partners | No, until Phase 2 |
| How do the university lab and the India and Canada teams split task-set and use-case construction? | Index lead | Yes |
| Which three use cases form the MVP, and which public tools do we mirror first? | Use-case owners | Yes |
| What is the production bar format: a single pass rate, or pass rate plus a cost ceiling? | Index lead with Risk | Yes |

**Phases.**

| Phase | Summary | Exit gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Decision spike, four weeks, before Phase 0 | Three real bounded workflows, three model classes, practitioner-set bars, run by hand with no harness | A first read on H1 at trivial cost; no bounded workflow clearing its bar on a smaller model re-scopes the TL Index to a regression gate and release-evaluation service |
| Phase 0, foundation | Harness on an open base, model register schema, cost and latency instrumentation; the judge panel and human sampling wait on the judge-location ruling (simulated Risk view at most); gateway telemetry classification moves to TL-009 | One public benchmark reproduced within published error bars |
| Phase 1, MVP on five pillars | Financial data processing, client care, entity recognition, compliance and regulatory, safety and grounding; practitioner-built task sets and signed production bars; one frontier model, two open-weight classes, one Enterprise specialist | First sufficiency map, a first H1/H3 read, one real model decision and one governance approval made through the index |
| Phase 2, full pillar set and first quarterly report | Remaining eleven pillars, deployability axis with post-quantization scores, business-line composites, IRT stitching, first independent audit of training batches | First Index report to leadership with a spend-impact estimate |
| Phase 3, operationalise | Regression gate in the model-swap process, release evaluation within ten working days, fine-tuning programme adopts the index, procurement scorecard, rotating held-out slices and the index-to-production correlation check each quarter |  |

Doc: [TL Index (index) — Project Proposal](https://claude.ai/artifact/4pwnZVstw8ZpcAP6sofN4U)

</details>

TL-010's purpose lives on as how an agent is packaged: a forkable reference build with the gateway, observability and evaluation wired in, since agents replaced blueprints in the portal. Carried from TL-012: Spec session (pre-Proposal changes from the Stress test pack): cap each purpose at two variants x two levels; write the judge stand-in calibration plan (frontier-panel judge from a different model line, human audit, published agreement); harden the after-call design (no CRM write credential in v1, protected fields excluded, injection test set, approved-with-error measure); declare each purpose's dataset and what it can prove; record the loop's governance cost under both answers to the variant question. Then move to Proposal. Building can start at week 8 (2026-12-28) with slots Tuesday and Friday, 30 minutes, if TL-004's week-4 gate (2026-12-04) is met; otherwise pre-review only. Quick call TL-012-q3 (kill-criteria read dates) on the dashboard From TL-003 (2026-11-07, D-178): the agent library's first entry is marketing pre-review, matching wave-1 order; the knowledge agent shows as coming next. From TL-007 (2026-11-08, D-204): D-051 still reads proposed although D-159 accepted it; add a line that marks it accepted so it leaves the 'awaiting your accept' count. Carried from TL-010 (blueprints): Seed until phase 2; the first Shaping session picks the first blueprint (RAG) and the enhance path for existing applications. From TL-012 (2026-11-02): each published variant is a blueprint candidate; wave 1 gives the first three

**Decisions due.**
_None._

**Hypotheses.**

| Claim | Confirmed if | Refuted if | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| H1 (company version). Admitted agent purposes cluster to the Domain candidates plus a platform set, with several purposes per domain sharing a model recipe | By the end of wave 1, servicing (Advice), Compliance and Judge each hold three or more admitted purposes with named owners | A domain holds one or fewer admitted purposes after a quarter; TL-011 should not carry it as a Domain model | open |
| H2 (lab, near-circular). Most multi-model agents are Core plus a judge, so the Judge family is the shared dependency and the first platform build | 70% or more of admitted multi-model purposes carry a judge component and the Judge family holds the highest demand count | Fewer than half carry one, or deterministic checks replace the judge in most | open |
| H3 (lab, proxy). Methodology variants of one purpose differ enough that the choice matters, and the simplest variant does not always win | For at least two purposes, variants differ by more than 5 rubric points, or by 2x on cost at equal quality | Variants within 3 points and 1.5x cost on all purposes: stop building variants, ship the simplest build (kill criterion I-5) | open |
| H4 (lab, provisional). The winning variant on TLM4 Core with the house judge matches a frontier baseline at lower cost, with data inside the boundary | Within 3 points of the frontier at half the cost or less | The frontier leads by more than 3 points after one retrain cycle; that purpose is not evidence for a Domain model | open |
| H5 (company version). A compared, adoptable variant is picked up by a business team without ThinkLatent engineering | Two or more teams adopt a wave-1 purpose within 60 days; first use inside two weeks | No adoption outside the originating team | open |
| H6 (company version). Wave 1 reduces human time on its two business purposes without losing recall | 30% less after-call time; 40% less reviewer time at no loss of recall | No change, or recall drops; the agent is withdrawn from that queue | open |
| H7 (lab, narrowed). A change of harness or serving configuration moves pass^k materially on one Core version; the Core-version comparison waits for the company version | The serving-configuration delta exceeds 5 pass^k points on at least two purposes | Serving-configuration deltas sit within noise; harness variants are not worth a stream | open |
| H8 (company version). Shown quality, cost, latency and reliability side by side, business teams choose variants on the measured frontier | 70% or more of choices sit on the accuracy-cost frontier | Choices cluster on a dominated variant; the comparison card is redesigned | open |

**Kill criteria.**

| Criterion | Checked | Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| I-1. A declared dataset for each purpose (public or synthetic, provenance written), with what it can and cannot prove | End of week 8 (2027-01-01) | Fewer than 2 of 3 purposes have one: park the purposes without one |
| I-2. TL-004 (TL Index) week-4 gate met, with S-E's first task set delivered | Read as a start condition at week 8 | Gate missed, or the task set missing because of TL-012: release S-E and cut wave 1 to one purpose (pre-review) |
| I-3. Judge stand-in agreement with a human-graded audit of 100 or more items per purpose | Week 10 | Agreement below 0.6: H3 and H4 become descriptive only and drive no publish decision |
| I-4. Weekly review slots for TL-012 kept by Jithin (Tuesday, Friday) | Rolling | 2 slots missed in a row: TL-012 leaves Building (D-152) |
| I-5. H3: paired variant deltas with 95% intervals | Week 12 | All purposes within 3 points and 1.5x cost, or intervals wider than +/-5 points: stop building variants and ship variant A only |
| I-6. Engineer-weeks and review hours burned against plan | Week 11 | More than 60% of either burned with no purpose past its first comparison: park at the circuit breaker, never extend |
| Pre-review: recall and precision on a frozen labelled set of 200 or more known violations | Set by end of week 8, read week 11 | No set in time: park. Recall below 0.95 or precision below 0.5: withdraw |
| Task mining: cluster stability across two seeds; clusters above the privacy threshold (set at the spike start) that map to a named Domain candidate | End of week 9 | Stability below 0.7 adjusted Rand, or fewer than 3 mappable clusters: kill the spike and read H1 from the admission ledger |
| After-call: (a) proposals touching protected fields on an injection test set; (b) variant A field accuracy against the frontier baseline; (c) TL-008 shaped | (c) by week 8, (a)(b) week 12 | (a) any such proposal: stop the purpose. (b) more than 5 points behind after the optimised level: park. (c) not shaped: variant A only |

**Kill signals.**

_None recorded._

**Open questions.**

_None recorded._

| D-236 | 2026-11-11 | Portal direction. Why we build it: to express the collective intelligence of the organisation, at institution level, by embedding it in models, which no frontier lab offers; once embedded, teams use the models for new use cases, those generate interaction data and feedback, and the business intelligence compounds. The portal must let people see that compounding without being told. The portal is organised around models and use cases, not roles: the model is the core pillar (foundation model, domain model built from it, use case on top, with versions that move together); the only thing tied to people is membership of a use case, given by adding a company email; everything the Head sees is open to everyone, including a leaderboard of use cases by usage and value. Every number links to the page behind it. The prototype is the full requirement for the next year: build everything, do not cut for timeline or role; hiding comes later as an option. Each request and each call inside it is a signal to capture and show. This supersedes the role workspaces of D-219 to D-222 and the role lens in D-234 | Jithin | accepted |
| D-237 | 2026-11-11 | Built and published on D-236: role switch removed; a space for every use case with people, invite by company email and an open view for non-members; command-centre leaderboard open to everyone (tasks, share, paid, avoided by the Finance method, model, hosting, contact); model-to-use-case flows; catalogue of foundation models only, each model's page listing every use case on it and the versions trained from it; TL Index overview with every model's score, best model per pillar and a model picker; the loop page following one turn record by record; the signals page listing what one request gives and which signals are not captured yet; the data loop (new kinds of data found, feedback shared back). Sample records for these were added to the scenario package; the counts of new data examples and the feedback rate are sample anchors, not measurements. Not done: a value index across use cases (no formula agreed), the remaining role-shaped pages (approvals, operations) are open to all but still worded for their old owners | Claude | accepted |

(2) The Index gets a category, Real Enterprise, built from every use case's own items, with a score per use case; the team writes the items (the ask is in enterprise-benchmark-team-ask-2026-11-11.md). Public benchmark samples are adopted on day one as the format and a smoke test. (3) What the serving backend logs (traces, sessions, scores) comes into the portal in the portal's own design, at use-case level and rolled up at model level. (4) Beyond performance, the portal watches the data flow itself: requests by size (tokens in against tokens out), what requests are about, entities, their trend, and a Watch agent that flags what moved. (5) References for the Index pages: a public model leaderboard, its single-model page and its trends page (frontier, open and TLM side by side); the model page lists every use case row by row with live numbers. | Jithin | accepted |

| D-249 | 2026-11-11 | Vision clarified in the build chat, one question at a time. It reaffirms D-236: models and projects, no roles; 'Preview as' and the role homes are not to be built (this reverses that part of D-248). (1) 'Model' means every kind of model, not only language models: TLM1-generation classifiers built on BERT, the speech-recognition network that processes about 150,000 calls a day, redaction, guardrails, embeddings, rerankers (Claude's reading of 'reversion model', not yet confirmed), and models that work in the background; more as applications evolve. (2) Many to many: a model serves many projects and one project uses several models, as different jobs in one flow, as separate features, as old and new side by side, and as agents that bring a model. (3) The project carries the stage and is delivered in increments: new models for new tasks are added at any time, increments run in parallel, and each goes to production on its own approval. (4) Each kind of model is scored on its own measure with a bar and a leaderboard of its kind; the TL Index stays for language models. (5) A shared model is handled both ways: Enterprise-wide work is one project for the Enterprise, and the same model can also sit inside another project for its own purpose (speech in an application's interface, redaction as a support function). (6) Hierarchy, in Jithin's words: Organization, Team, Project, Feature (for example Retail Business, Advice Tech, Advisor desktop, then call summary, next best action, speech to text; the same team can hold a second project), with models under features. The names and where membership sits are still being settled. Where a board assumes roles or one model per project, this vision wins and the board guides layout only (amends D-247). | Jithin | accepted |
| D-250 | 2026-11-11 | Vision, continued (follows D-249). (1) Levels: Organization, Team, Project, Feature, with models under features; a project carries its team as owner and can move teams without losing its history (Claude's caution, not yet confirmed). (2) Membership: people join a project; a team's owners can open and manage every project the team holds; everyone else gets the open view. (3) Rule for the whole navigation: every level shows the breakdown of the level below it, and the detail (traces, sessions, operations) lives at the project; a model's page shows improvement, API calls and tokens, adoption and value. (4) Two-level navigation, option A, chosen from three drawn options (canvas 'navigation options'): the approved dock stays and gets shorter; a clickable scope bar says where you are (Organization, Team, Project, Feature); tabs under the title change with the level. (5) Home is a cockpit, not one list: TLM model adoption and what is running in production today by stage; usage by kind; an adoption and usage leaderboard at team level with a double-click into a team's projects; and the AI agent in front, which understands the ask, brings the information and routes the person to the right place, flattening the path from onboarding to approvals. (6) Jithin's ask for what follows: Claude keeps asking questions one at a time until the vision is clear, then designs the UX and UI in the approved design and draws it by hand so it fits; nothing more is built until then. | Jithin | accepted |
| D-251 | 2026-11-11 | Vision, continued (follows D-250). (1) Usage by kind of work comes off Home for now and is parked for later as a Home exhibit; Home keeps models live by kind and requests split into live and background. (2) Approvals sit outside the portal, for projects and for models alike. A deployment triggers a workflow in the Enterprise's change system and the change request is approved by the ThinkLatent team; model approval is also recorded in an outside system. The portal prepares the evidence, raises the request and shows its status; it approves nothing. The approver queues and hold-to-approve screens for use cases and validations on the earlier boards become request status. (3) The model catalogue is grouped by generation first (TLM1 to TLM5), with the kinds of model inside each. (4) Budgets are held by the team; spend and value are counted per project and feature and roll up to team and organization. (5) Taken as settled unless Jithin says otherwise: the agent on Home is one line at the top of the cockpit (Claude's recommendation after Jithin asked for analysis); 'reversion model' is read as reranker. | Jithin | accepted |

| D-259 | 2026-11-12 | The page that replaces approvals, 'Requests and their status', tracks four kinds of request on one page: deployment change requests (an increment going to Pilot or Production, decided in the Enterprise's change system by the ThinkLatent team), access requests (joining a project is settled by its owners in the portal; production access is granted in IAM), asks to ThinkLatent (a new project, a new model or feature, training capacity) and problems logged (each a ticket, listed openly). | D-260 | 2026-11-12 | A feature has two bars: a minimum set by the ThinkLatent team for each kind of task (as the 85% safety minimum already is, on every feature), and the project team's own target above it. The project page shows both bars per feature. Proposed by Claude and still to be confirmed: a change request cannot be raised while a score is under its minimum, and can be raised with a note when it is only under the team's target. Jithin also asked that questions come through the question prompt, with a recommendation, so that he is notified. | Jithin | accepted |
| D-261 | 2026-11-12 | What a feature is held to is two kinds of bar on two different measures, corrected by Jithin. The TL Index thresholds come from the ThinkLatent team, because the project uses a model from ThinkLatent's list that is already scored on the Index. The project team sets what it expects of the application's own quality. After training, the project team may go ahead under a TL Index threshold: that is a risk the team accepts, it is flagged on the feature and the project, and the deviation travels with the evidence so that governance, which approves outside the portal, sees it. Claude's earlier line that a change request cannot be raised under a minimum is withdrawn. For application quality the portal gives teams default ways of scoring to choose from (exact match, F1 score, closeness to a reference, a model as judge with standard judges, a model as judge with the team's own rubric, people scoring a sample) and one data format their evaluation set must match, with a template and a check on every upload. The feature page is redrawn to this and a feature's evaluation set page is drafted; the field names in the format are Claude's proposal for the team to confirm. | Jithin | accepted |
| D-262 | 2026-11-12 | Two more answers. A project team's own judge must be checked against answers the team scored by hand before its scores count as evidence, and the agreement is shown with the evidence. The guided next step lives in the project and is listed in My work; there is no separate Journey item in the dock, and the agent can walk a person through any step. My work is drafted on this, with no roles: a short account from the agent of what changed since the person was last here, the next step in each of their projects with the project's stage, the requests that need them and those they wait on, what they follow, and what they opened recently. | D-301 | 2026-11-13 | Jithin asked that Claude put its assumptions to him as questions with a recommendation, not assume, so that both are on the same page; twelve were asked and answered. On prompts: registering a version sends its text to the portal, where the project's members can read it; a team's prompt joins the library after ThinkLatent has checked and scored it. On training: a stage's check is scored on the whole TL Index; a base may lose 2 points on any live pillar before a run stops; two checkpoints are kept for each stage, the best and the last, in place of Claude's rule of keeping others for 30 days; a judge used in training may also score a model on the TL Index, where Claude had drawn a rule against it (the rule and its checks are removed from the boards); the Data tab becomes four pages, with the data sets on their own. On where a run lives, in his words: training runs as Slurm jobs on HyperPod, a dedicated S3 bucket holds the runs, each under a run id; during training things are brought into the FSx area of the cluster for fast access, and a checkpoint that beats the best is written to the run's S3 prefix, so that lineage, the data used and the configuration are all in the run's folder; a model good enough for consumption is promoted to a standard place. He chose pointers to the data, not copies, and asked what happens if the store changes during a run: versions never change once cut, a run trains on the copy it took at its start, and for the one real change, a withdrawal, the run finishes and the foundation lead then decides. Release is one step: once the TL Index run confirms the model works it is released, the projects on it are told, and the portal runs each project's own evaluation ahead of them so the team can decide; this puts release before the projects' evaluations, where the boards had them the other way round. Recipes are kept in the portal, with standard recipes defined by ThinkLatent at the start and new ones built from the methods available; a run freezes its recipe into its folder (this replaces 'a file in the TLM package'). A run's folder is kept 180 days after the run ends, and the small record of a run that produced a released model travels with the model. A run's id is readable and meaningful: model, what the run is for (the project where there is one) and date; Claude set the form as model, purpose, date and a letter. Written: a requirement for AI engineering, 'Training runs: where a run lives, the process and the folder for each run id' (the four places, the run id, the folder tree, the FSx layout, the released model's folder, twelve steps with who writes what, what happens if data changes, what is kept, and five open choices each with a recommendation). Drawn or changed: a run's files (new), the data sets on their own page (new), the run page (two checkpoints, the path to a release in the new order), the recipe and Training pages (standard recipes, kept in the portal), the model's own Training page (release order), the Data pages (no judge rule, four pages). TL Index upkeep: a new edition each month, signed off by the Head of ThinkLatent; a sixth of each edition is new (200 questions) and 1,000 are carried; stage checks in training use their own set of 1,200 questions from the pool that are never in an edition, so that a checkpoint is never picked on the questions that later grade the model (this replaces 'the whole TL Index' for stage checks in D-301); anyone may ask for a model to be scored and ThinkLatent accepts, in the order release first, re-scoring after a correction, accepted asks by age, practice runs when machines are free, with a training run's checks shown to the foundation team only. Three further questions went unanswered and Claude drew its recommendation for each, to be confirmed: a question that leaves an edition goes into the stage-check set and is then retired, one way only; the upkeep page is for ThinkLatent only, with the edition's page and the queue open to everyone; outside models are scored again with every edition and at once when a vendor changes a model. Drawn (first drafts): TL Index upkeep (the pool by pillar, a question's life, the next edition step by step, corrections), an edition's open page (models against the last edition on the carried questions, what changed, how it was signed off, the open list of corrections, the editions), and the evaluation queue (the order, what runs and waits, one run live with provisional scores, who sees what). The Data pages gained the Entity recognition evaluation set they lacked: 17 data sets, 5 for evaluation, 4,500 TL Index questions and 500 head-to-head pairs. Against the recommendation: a new edition every two weeks, not every four, with 100 new questions each time (20 a pillar) and corrections going in with it; the Head of ThinkLatent signs each one, so twice a month (this replaces the monthly edition with a sixth new in D-302 and the separate corrections run drawn in D-303). As recommended: a training run's check shows in the open queue as a nameless 'ThinkLatent · internal run'; a project may offer a version of its own evaluation set to the Index, which is profiled by the portal, checked blind by practitioners and accepted or declined by the pillar council, with the contributor credited and its own model never scored on what it gave; and no quote is asked from the outside benchmark company for now, its free public samples being run only as a smoke test. Claude named the second edition of a month with a letter (2026-11b on 10 Nov). Drawn or changed: upkeep, the edition's page and the queue reworked for two-weekly editions; the sign-off as the Head sees it while it waits, on upkeep; a set offered by a project (the offer, the profile's nine checks, the practitioner check, the proposal for the council, offers so far), reached from a feature's evaluation set. One consequence drawn rather than decided: real calls may not be sent to outside models, so the Index agent proposes a cap of 5 such questions among an edition's 20 new ones. He also asked Claude to remember to ask questions directly, each with a recommendation, so that work continues (saved to the Project's memory). Drawn or changed: upkeep, the edition's page and the queue now name the Index team and show delta scoring (a model answers only what changed; a changed model, ours or a vendor's, gets a full run); a Trends page (the best outside, ThinkLatent and open model over 13 editions, whether each pillar still tells models apart, the forecast, the quarterly report). Seven points he did not answer are drawn on Claude's recommendation and stay open for him: a question leaving an edition goes to the stage-check set and then retires; the upkeep page is ThinkLatent only; at most 5 real calls among an edition's 20 new questions; the Index team and the foundation team may both open a question (Claude recommends the Index team only); the Index agent watches saturation and the Index team hardens, with retiring a pillar left to the pillar council; trends are open and the forecast shows to ThinkLatent only; the quarterly report is drafted by the Index agent and signed by the Head. | D-306 | 2026-11-13 | Eleven answers on the TL Index, 13 Nov night, which close the points D-305 left open. | D-463 | 2026-11-21 | TL-003 portal (the portal app): Jithin, 21 Nov, his direction for the redesign after colour v3, to think through and not build yet: (1) the user flow and the order of things are not right; moving between places is unclear and a screen offers too many things to do; (2) remove the in-page section bar (the index of the page): it competes with the sub-menu at the top; (3) too much text; it is not clear what is a button and what is a link; (4) the portal is where people start a project and use models in it, so pages should lead with what a person does (forms, submitting, a next action), not with charts; on Training, Usage and live production the chart is not the main content; (5) remove no feature: where a page holds too much, split it into a flow of pages, with an entry page that shows what can be done here and takes the person in to act; (6) assistants: one panel that slides in from the right over a blurred page, answers and generated pieces shown on a canvas beside it (as in Claude Design), modals that blur the page, and generative UI for the onboarding agent; (7) Home first as the trial, after he decides what it leads with; questions with recommendations to come when he is ready. ### 2026-11-07 · Spec · v6 batch 1 (onboarding to production)

- **Register drift fixed.** The 5 and 6 Nov sessions recorded decisions 11 to 16, the Stress test close and the answers given during the rename only in the claude.ai docs. They are now D-142 to D-149, with the stage history (Stress test and Proposal, 6 Nov) and the critique flag corrected.
- **Batch 1 published** on the canvas page "v6 · batch 1: onboarding to production", in journey order:
  1. Start a project (J0)
  2. Project home (J2)
  3. Usage, keys and first call (J10)
  4. New training run (T1, a stepper)
  5. Runs in flight (T2)
  6. Checkpoints (T6)
  7. Evaluation results (E1)
  8. Approval UA-0291 (V1)
  9. Models and canary (J6)
  10. Use case overview in production (J3)

  Home and the model page were brought to decision 16 too. Together the pages cover 35 v5.1 boards, which stay on the canvas until Jithin approves the batch.
- **How it was built.** One brief (`v6-brief`: anatomy, text budget, tooltip budget, scores, precision, names, links) went to eight parallel agents, one page each, plus one for the pilots. A lint checked names, the tooltip budget, links and anchors. Per page, tooltips now run 1 to 8 in column headers, 0 in table cells and 8 to 15 elsewhere (the pilots had 18 to 35, plus up to 14 in cells).
- **For the review: numbers that disagree or are missing.**
  - V1 kept its source's run 1183 pillar scores, which disagree with E1. Safety and grounding is 91% ± 2 in V1's source and 78% ± 4 in E1, and E1's figure would fail the 85% bar.
  - J6's paired ± values were worked out from the source's p-values, not copied from given intervals.
  - Paired intervals are still missing in four places, which show verdicts without ± or read "ungraded" rather than invented values:
    - E1's pillar table (run 1183 against 1162)
    - M2's profile against Core 4.1
    - J3's Distil 4.2 tie
    - T6's checkpoint changes
  - V1 follows J3's consistency figure (71% against a 75% bar), not its source's 64% against 70%.
- **Next batches.** About 146 v5.1 boards remain, in three or four batches. Batch 2 is the live use case: Performance, Quality, Improve, Record and alerts.
- **Audit, same day.** A separate agent checked the batch against the v5.1 boards; it had not seen how the pages were made. Its main finding: run 1183's Safety and grounding is 78% ± 4, not the 91% ± 2 shown on the approval page. So UA-0291 misses its 85% bar, and the approval page now recommends sending it back with two findings.
- **Corrected after the audit:**
  - Run 1180 now uses the Official leaderboard scores on all four pages.
  - The unsourced activity table on Home is removed.
  - Models and canary no longer shows ± worked out from p-values; it shows the difference and p instead.
  - Verdicts that rested only on overlapping intervals now read "paired test pending".
- **Left for review:**
  - Distil 4.2's scores against run 1179
  - the Core 4.1 column on the model page
  - approval check C-2.4

  The mock data disagrees across the v5.1 boards themselves. The seeded scenario generator (decision 14) fixes that by construction.

### 2026-11-07 (evening) · Spec · scenario generator; batch 1 regenerated

- **Decisions.** D-177: Cortex stays a separate app and plugin; the core-team, Jira and bridge boards leave the portal. D-178: pre-review is the agent library's first entry. D-179: a pending paired test blocks approval (C-2.4). D-180: the 85% Safety gate is kept on every use case. D-181 and D-182: the generator is built and batch 1 is regenerated.
- **Generator.** `thinklatent-labs/portal`, `packages/scenario`. The anchors (the leaderboard's run scores, model facts, use-case bars, policy floors, approval checks) feed one seed. It builds item-level scores, bootstrap intervals, paired tests, sufficiency and approval checks.
  - `pnpm scenario` writes `out/scenario.json` and `out/facts.md`. `pnpm lint:boards <dir>` checks boards.
  - Ten tests cover determinism, the anchors, the bar rule, the verdicts and both stories.
- **Audit.** A separate agent checked all 12 pages and found the core numbers matching. Of its 21 items, 15 are fixed. Three wait for generator v0.2: use-case inventory, finance method and token volumes. The rest are small review items, listed in the row's next step.
- **The consequence to weigh.** At an 85% Safety floor, the best Official score is the frontier API's 84%. No sample project can reach production until a model clears it.

### 2026-11-16 · Portfolio · the portal becomes a programme (merges)

Jithin asked to merge what links to the portal into it, so the portal is one programme with its backends in view. He chose the merge rule (D-387): a merged row closes at Merged, and its purpose and every open item move to a track here.

- **TL Index** (`TL-003.index`, from TL-004, D-388). Building, decision spike from 16 Nov, first Friday review 20 Nov. Its Friday 30-minute slot is now the portal's weekly review, which closes the slot gap of D-326 and D-327. D-155 stays: the index judges apart from TLM model-building.
- **Telemetry contract** (`TL-003.telemetry`, from TL-009, D-389). Seed. The Langfuse field audit runs inside the Enterprise with the fast-lane proof, by 27 Nov; a short critique comes before the contract is built.
- **Agents library** (`TL-003.agents`, from TL-012 and TL-010, D-390). Stress test, as TL-012 was. TL-012 joined on Jithin's call, against the recommendation to wait for its Proposal. Its Spec session is due 21 Nov; nothing builds before the TL Index track's week-4 gate on 4 Dec.

The harness and the fast lane went to TL-018 instead (D-392); the portal lists them as backend dependencies. Of the nine backends the portal reads from, five run in production, self-healing is built, and three are only proposed (the evaluation harness, the contribution flow, the harness runtime). Until those land the portal runs on sample data, and the tracks are where they get checked.

Honest read: one programme can hide very different work behind one review. Each track keeps its own owner, dates and kill criteria so that does not happen; the dashboard now flags the portal leaning on a Seed-stage telemetry track.
